e:["$","p",null,{"children":[["$","code",null,{"children":"flops.as_symmetric()"}]," validates the data first. Validation uses a tolerance,\nwhile the tag it grants is read as exact — the cost model prices every position\nin an orbit after the first as redundant and does not read the buffer again — so\n",["$","code",null,{"children":"as_symmetric"}]," makes the two agree before tagging: each orbit takes the value at\nits lexicographically smallest index. Data that is already exactly invariant is\nreturned unchanged, so a genuinely symmetric array is tagged without being\ncopied; data that passed only within the tolerance is copied first, and the\nvalues in the discarded positions do not survive. Your own array is never\nmodified either way."]}]

2d:["$","p",null,{"children":["Both functions also accept ",["$","code",null,{"children":"mode=\"canonical-copy\""}],", which keeps one entry per\norbit — the one at the lexicographically smallest index — instead of averaging\nthe orbit. It replaces the ",["$","code",null,{"children":"|G| * n + n"}]," projection term with a single ",["$","code",null,{"children":"n"}],"\ncopy pass (so ",["$","code",null,{"children":"flops.symmetrize"}]," costs ",["$","code",null,{"children":"n"}],", and ",["$","code",null,{"children":"fnp.random.symmetric"}],"\ncosts ",["$","code",null,{"children":"C_dist(n) + n"}],"), preserves the input dtype instead of promoting to\nfloat64, and reads only the group's generators, so it stays available for groups\ntoo large to enumerate (see below). It is a different result, not a cheaper\nroute to the same one: the other entries in each orbit are discarded rather than\naveraged in. Reach for it when you want a tensor that ",["$","em",null,{"children":"has"}]," the symmetry, and\nfor Reynolds when you want the projection of your data ",["$","em",null,{"children":"onto"}]," the invariant\nsubspace."]}]
